                      Natural product inspired antibiotics approved for
                human use  1943 to 2025                       –
            Cite this: DOI: 10.1039/d5np00067j

                               Mark S. Butler   and Robert J. Capon   *

PM.                                          Covering literature to September 2025

                                               This review provides a comprehensive account of the 217 natural product inspired antibiotics that have been2:15:48                                      approved for human use from 1943 through to September 2025, inclusive of 52 (24%) that are natural
                                          products (NPs) and 165 (76%) that are semi-synthetic or synthetic derivatives of natural products (NP-
                                                  Ds). These are organized into sixteen categories deﬁned by shared structural motifs and, in many cases,12/12/2025                             common mechanisms of action. Each antibiotic  is classiﬁed as either a NP or NP-D, annotated by
on                                       a molecular structure that, where relevant, highlights the relationships between NPs and NP-Ds. Market
                                                    details are also provided, including the company that brought each antibiotic to market, the year and
                                           country of ﬁrst approval, the spectrum of usage across pathogen classes, routes of administration,

                                             current status, and selected commentary on mechanisms of action. The assembled dataset is furtherDownloaded
                                            analysed through a series of charts that illustrate insightful trends that document the remarkable history
           Received 1st October 2025
                                      and lasting impact of NP inspired antibiotics. The review concludes with observations on the historic2025.
          DOI: 10.1039/d5np00067j
                                         impact and future prospects of natural products as a source of inspiration for the development of new

                                                    17.  Fidaxomicin

                                                                   Fidaxomicin (17.01) (tiacumicin B) is a glycosylated polyketide
                                                                    macrolide whose structure (Table 42 and Fig. 43) was reported
                                                                  by Abbott Laboratories in 1987 from the actinomycete Dactylo-
                                                                    sporangium aurantiacum subsp. hamdenensis.1019,1020 Around the
                                                          same time, this structure was also assigned to clostomicin B1
                                                            and  lipiarmycin  A3   (rst  reported  in  1975  without
                                                                   a structure).1021–1024 Fidaxomicin exhibits high selectivity for C.
                                                                            diﬃcile over other G+ve bacteria, with minimal or no activity
                                                                          against G−ve bacteria, thereby minimising disruption to the gut
                                                                               microbiota.1025 Fidaxomicin inhibits RNA synthesis by binding

                                                                                                state.1026–1029 Clinical resistance remains rare, with reduced
